# LEDGER — 3D fill audit lane units (secretary-maintained)

Maintained by the lane secretary. One row per unit in the handoff queue
(`docs/3d-audit/fill-audit-handoff.md` §Queue). Status vocabulary:
`QUEUED | IN-FLIGHT | DONE | DONE/FU | MEASURED | REVERTED | BLOCKED | NEEDS-ORCH-LOOK | REJECTED`.
Initial statuses seeded 2026-09-05 from the handoff doc; updated on each "reports in" message.
Evidence path = `docs/3d-audit/fill-audit/after/<W-id>/` unless noted. Report files live beside
this ledger as `<W-id>-<role>.md`.

| **F1-placement** (the user's "not close to zero" complaint) | fill-audit-a — `surface-fill.js` | **PLAN-READY** — `F1-placement-plan.md` (MEASURED: mechanism found, located to file:line, **prototyped twice with before/after numbers**). **ORDER on fill-audit-a: W-26b → F1-placement → T2 → T3** — F1 goes ahead of T2 | — | — | — | **The plan overturns the A3 judge's routing: F1 is a RULING-PLACEMENT defect, not "between ribbon stretches", and it is NOT pen-unreachable — and it is the SAME mechanism as W-26's root cause.** `weightCovAt` (`:4273`) falls through to `weightBaseCov()` (`:4303`) → `wvFlatCov()` (`:3910`), which **takes no arguments** — one constant for the whole object, no radiance term — and drives the Sturmian accumulator `ladderStep` (`:1289`, seeded `LADDER_PHASE0 = 0.5`). At d=50 `c = 0.44803`, so kept-index gaps are **{2×7, 3×2}**: one gap in four is **50% wider with literally zero tone behind it**. ### Unit D phase-align (8e9b0991, handoff-c) — forwarded 2026-09-05, review in flight
Secretary pre-checks that PASSED: correct lane files only, `hlr.js` untouched, the `VECTURA_PRE_UNITD_PHASE`
test hook is confined to `tests/unit/scene3d-shadow-receive.test.js` and does **not** appear in production
`shadows.js` (grepped `src/`), the original non-memo branch is untouched byte-for-byte, RED reproduced by
pinning the pre-fix file, and the gallery correctly not rebuilt (per the standing ruling; no cell reaches
the flag anyway).
Flags to forward:
1. **The fix is a module-level `WeakMap` side-channel keyed by ring OBJECT IDENTITY**, and it works only
   because `scene3d.js` passes the *same* array objects both into the outside `.concat` and later as
   `[fp]`. If anyone ever inserts a defensive copy, a `.map()`, or a serialization round-trip on
   `footprintPolys`, the memo silently misses and the phase reverts to the buggy behaviour **with no test
   failure** — unless the new test drives the real `scene3d.js` footprint path rather than calling
   `hatchRingsEvenOdd` with hand-built arrays. **Confirm which it does**; that is the difference between a
   regression guard and a snapshot.
2. **Order-dependent, never-cleared module state.** The memo is written only on `rings.length > 1` calls
   and read on `rings.length === 1` calls, so it assumes the outside call always precedes the inside ones.
   Ask what happens if a render skips or reorders the outside call, or if the same poly object survives
   into a later render with a changed angle/spacing — can a stale anchor be used?
3. **A notable reframing of the original defect, worth recording:** the implementer tried a real-browser
   screenshot first and abandoned it because the "broken dash" appearance showed up in **both** before and
   after and proved to be a rasterization/upscale alias — confirmed by dumping `g.scenePaths`, where every
   receiver fill path is a single unbroken 2-point chord in both states. So the phase irregularity the
   judge measured was real, but the "reads as dashes" description was partly an artifact. Final evidence
   renders emitted paper-space paths as crisp SVG via Playwright instead.
4. **`scripts/unitd-phase-evidence.js` swaps `shadows.js` on disk** to obtain a genuine before, and claims
   to restore it unconditionally with an on-disk check after every run. Verify the failure/exception paths
   restore too — a committed script that mutates a source file is a footgun for the next session.
5. **Fourth gallery coverage gap today:** no cell exercises `shadowReceiveOnObjects` (a scene-level flag,
   off everywhere), so there is nothing to md5 and the evidence is necessarily a bespoke scene.

### T1 (67c9752c, fill-audit-a) — forwarded 2026-09-05, review in flight (pinned 3c88605f..67c9752c)
Secretary pre-checks that PASSED: correct 2 files only, no forbidden file touched, O3's RED reproduces
the plan's table to 3 decimals (strong evidence the fixture and methodology are faithful), and the three
shortfall bars were set at the honestly measured floor rather than the plan's number restated as green.
Flags to forward:
1. **The kink episode is the most important thing in this report and should shape the review.** The
   first implementation drew a visible chevron/V at the centre of every tick — a herringbone, not a tick
   texture — and **all four O1–O4 oracles passed while it did**, with O1's sagitta *higher* (0.377 mm)
   because the kink was read as curvature. It was caught by looking, then fixed. **So O1 in particular
   cannot distinguish curvature from a defect**; the reviewer should ask what now would fail if a kink
   returned, and look at the pictures rather than the numbers.
2. **Three of four oracles ship below the plan's own bars** — O1 0.127 vs ≥0.15, O2 0.162/0.109 vs ~0.01,
   O4 0.809 vs ≥0.95. The implementer's reasons are specific and testable rather than hand-waved: the
   plan's RED came from *internal* frame access, the file's existing `nearestRulingTangent` proxy
   saturates at a spurious 90° on **both** trees (pre-fix 83% over 10° by that proxy versus the plan's
   bounded internal 44.77°), and genuine surface curvature over a 2.25–8.7 mm tick arm legitimately puts
   a chord more than 10° from a flat reference — "a metric built against a flat reference cannot
   simultaneously reward curvature and demand near-zero deviation from a flat line". **That argument, if
   accepted, means O1/O2 as written are the wrong oracles and should be restated, not just missed.**
3. **O2 was restated, not merely missed**: "p99 ≤ 10°" became "fraction of marks over 10° ≤ 0.01",
   claimed exactly equivalent, then shipped at ≤0.20. Confirm the restatement is equivalent and that
   0.20 is the measured floor plus a small margin, not a convenient round number.
4. **A structural pin changed**: `expect(pp.length).toBe(2)` → `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2)`. Correct — the
   2-point pin *was* defect D1 — but it removes the only assertion that a mark's point count is bounded.
   Ask for an upper bound, so an unbounded walk cannot pass.
5. **O4's shortfall was deliberately left** per the plan's §9 stop conditions (truncate more ink versus
   widen the drop floor is not this unit's call). Confirm that trade-off is T2/T3's, and that the plan
   agrees, before it silently becomes nobody's.
6. Note the lane context: T1 sits on 3c88605f, so the **W-26b blocking unit is now editing the same file
   behind it**. The judge's verdict applies to 3c88605f as committed; T1 is the working-tree edit that
   judge flagged.

### W-27c-0a iteration 3 (ec79e2b9, fill-audit-d) — forwarded 2026-09-06, review in flight
Secretary pre-checks that PASSED: the mechanism was **replaced rather than tuned**; the user-reported
regression was verified gone **at native resolution** (Pillow crops, no upscale), which is how the
regression was found in the first place; the 0(b) ceiling came **back down** to ≤55; and every bar that
could not be held was **stop-reported with numbers instead of widened** — including four that iteration 2b
had asserted as passing.
Flags to forward:
1. **This is a partial ROLLBACK of iteration 2b's measured gains, and the report says so.** Torus waist
   0.216 mm (2b) → **0.054 mm** (iter-3) against a required ≥0.8w; sphere waist is **identical to RED**,
   zero improvement; largestW misses 2b's own floors on both primitives. The whole-ring mechanism fixes
   the regression but gives back ground on (c) and (d). **The orchestrator must decide what the unit's
   contract now is** — iteration 2b's floors can no longer all be held simultaneously.
2. **Ink retention fell to 70.8% on the torus** (from 94.9% at K=0.8 in iteration 2). The plan's
   acceptance band was ~20% loss; 29.2% is outside it. Confirm this is acceptable for whole-ring
   dropping, or the cull is removing rings that carry tone.
3. **Four assertions became measured-and-logged only.** Honest, but it means the file no longer fails if
   those quantities regress. Ask for floors at the iteration-3 values (the pattern 2b itself used) so the
   new position is defended, rather than leaving four quantities unguarded.
4. **The waist metric is questioned by the implementer** — "a single tight closest-approach POINT, not a
   sustained band". If that is right, O2(c) is the wrong oracle and should be restated rather than
   stop-reported indefinitely; if it is wrong, the waist is a real unfixed defect. Worth settling.
5. Positive worth confirming independently: the **sphere blob-count regression from iteration 2 (47 → 54)
   is not reproduced** (47 → 24). That was the disclosed regression that most worried the last review.

### U1 → U5 chain (8610fd66, fill-collapse) — forwarded 2026-09-06, review in flight
Secretary pre-checks that PASSED: five separate commits, one per cluster, serial as the plan required;
every bar change disclosed with file:line and old→new in each unit's `report.json` `bars_changed`; the
three integration tests broken at U4 were **re-fixtured onto a caveat-bearing law the plan never folds
(`bundleSubNib`), not loosened**; U0's two stale tests rewritten with before/after in the commit bodies.
Flags to forward:
1. **Two REAL regressions, correctly refused rather than patched over.** `bundleDither` (U4) and
   `contFieldTouch` (U5) are LIBRARY-tier laws with genuine measured caveats, and `fillStyleControls`
   renders its caveat line from the **resolved survivor id** — which has no caveat of its own — so
   **a user who picks those options no longer sees the warning anywhere in the UI.** Fixing it needs a
   UI-file change, an explicit stop condition for this data-only chain. **It will recur at U6**
   (`penStipple`'s caveat situation is adjacent). Needs either the UI follow-up (resolve the caveat from
   the fully-resolved internal id while keeping the (i) popover on the survivor — the two are conflated
   today) or a product sign-off that the loss is acceptable.

   shows the **wrong (default) sub-control value** on load or live-compose, though the render is correct.
   Same fix site, likely the same follow-up unit.
3. **The audit's C-05 "byte-identical" claim is disproved by live computation** — and the fix is **U8's
   job per the plan's docs contract, not done here**. If U8 is descoped or deferred, `worklist.json` and
   `findings.json` keep a claim this chain has proven false.
4. Watch at review: with `COLLAPSE` now non-empty, U0's deferred items come due — whether the derived
   `persistentStyleKeys()` can drop or reorder one of the base 3 keys (a persistence surface), and
   whether U0's monkey-patched synthetic-alias tests were replaced by real-data cases or left alongside.

   the chain reports "live-verified".

### W-26b (7bc2b1a0 / 4a858445 / 0930cb2d, fill-audit-a) - forwarded 2026-09-06, review in flight
Secretary pre-checks that PASSED: three separate commits, one per condition; the judge's prescribed bar
shape (floor **below** the envelope *plus* a fingerprint band) adopted exactly rather than reinterpreted;
W-26b-4a folded in and 4(b) correctly left record-only; C1's numbers reproduce the judge's own inkMm
figures to the millimetre; native-resolution crops used for the visual check.
Flags to forward:
1. **`dfMax` had to widen for the crossing family (`dfMaxMul`)** - the coverage-share fix alone left
   `crossDensityRatio`'s spread at ~1.6x, because the walk's own floor clamped the wider share-driven
   pitch straight back down. That is a **second lever pulled inside a blocking fix**; confirm it is
   scoped to the crossing family and cannot loosen the floor for anything else.
2. **The C2 near-miss should be verified, not just admired**: the first anti-blob guard used a
   disc-radius-relative grid, which fattened strokes and drove **every** mapper - including the healthy
   `hatch` - to coverage 1.0, i.e. it would have passed while measuring nothing. It was caught only
   because `hatch@50` reading 1.0 is impossible for a 67-line drawing. **Ask the reviewer to confirm the
   shipped absolute-mm instrument cannot saturate**, since this guard is the whole point of C2.
3. **Ellipsoid crosshatch D220 came out 15.6% LIGHTER than the true pre-W-26 baseline** - inside the cap
   as an absolute value, but a swing in the opposite direction from the defect. Check it is the share
   arithmetic landing correctly and not a new under-ink on that primitive.
4. C1 changes render behaviour, so it needs the **full guard battery**, not just the four named files -
   confirm W-26's own seven re-pinned files were re-run, since W-26b sits directly on top of them.
5. Docs: C1 makes the judge's **"avoid crosshatch above about Density 100" caveat liftable** per the
   standing ruling; update the for-Jay text and the CHANGELOG line in the same pass.

## Standing orchestrator rulings

- **A SUITE COUNT IN A MERGE REVIEW MUST QUOTE THE RUN'S OWN SUMMARY LINE, FROM A RUN THE REVIEWER COMPLETED IN THAT SESSION** (2026-09-19, Incident 17). **`MERGE-review-r4.md` claimed "all five suites green"; the reviewer had been killed mid-`test:unit` and, on resume, reported the step it had intended to finish. The pre-push hook then found FOUR RED FILES on merged `main`.** ⚠ **This is not dishonesty — it is what a resumed agent does with an intent it formed before the kill — and no amount of care fixes it, because the agent believes the claim. The only defence is QUOTING OUTPUT rather than summarising it: paste the `Test Files … passed (…)` line.** **Applies to every merge review, and to any unit report claiming a full-suite result.**
- **A DISCLOSED MISS MUST STILL LEAVE THE FILE GREEN — re-derive the bar WITH PROOF, or stop-report the unit. NEVER ship a red test on a merge candidate** (2026-09-19, from T2-6's O1 2 % miss). **T2-6 disclosed the miss honestly and shipped it red anyway; it then sat through a review and a merge before a git hook noticed.** ⚠ **Disclosure earns a unit the right to a RULING, not the right to leave a failing assertion in the tree** — a red test on `main` is indistinguishable from a regression to everyone who did not read the report, and this audit has spent four rounds establishing that reports are not where truth lives.

- **AN EDGE-GEOMETRY CHANGE MUST RUN EVERY PINNED-GOLDEN FILE OVER CURVED PRIMITIVES BEFORE COMMIT — GREP, DON'T RECALL** (2026-09-18, from W-32r4's REJECT). **The unit's fix, oracle, evidence and both DISCLOSED re-pins all reproduced exactly; it was rejected because the TREE WAS NOT GREEN** — `scene3d-facet-min-rulings` (12), `scene3d-box-density-bearing` (≥1) and `scene3d-mktick-wedge` (8) were RED, pinned sphere/cone goldens that **moved with the refined edge, were never run and were never disclosed.** ⚠ **The failure was not honesty — it was RECALL: the implementer ran the guards it thought of.** **A silhouette or edge change touches EVERY curved primitive's rendered output, so the guard list must be DERIVED BY GREP (pinned hashes, `toBe('<md5>')`, golden arrays) rather than assembled from a brief's named list or from memory.** ⚠ **And note the cost asymmetry: grepping is minutes; this cost a full REJECT on a unit whose mechanism was correct.**

- **⚙ PROCESS REGIME CHANGE — JAY'S RULING, 2026-09-15: THE AGENT ROSTER IS OVER-LEVELLED. Binding from now.** The round has been producing more process per unit than the units justify. **Six rules:**
  1. **The orchestrator batches messages to the secretary — once per two or three events.** The secretary replies **"RECORDED" plus ONLY decision lines whose answer changes what runs next** — not every open question, only the ones that gate the next start. *(Most of this round's DECISION lines were interesting rather than blocking.)*
  2. **"Secretary flags" are produced ONLY for units that change `src/`, and are CAPPED AT SIX.** A tests-only unit gets none. *(Nine and ten flags were being written for units that touched one test file.)*
  3. **TESTS-ONLY UNITS GET A LIGHT VERIFY PASS, NOT AN ADVERSARIAL REVIEW — three conditions: the RED reproduces, the mutation trips, and `## Bars changed` is accurate.** They are marked **VERIFIED**, not reviewed. *(W-36d, T4b, T4c and F1-width-bar each drew a full adversarial review; every one of them was ACCEPT, and their findings were follow-ups rather than defects.)*
  4. **NO PLANNERS unless a unit was REJECTED or its mechanism is UNKNOWN. T3 goes straight to an implementer.** *(T2-3 and W-31b earned their planners — both were post-rejection or mechanism-unknown. Plans written for units with a known mechanism and a clear path bought little the implementer could not have derived.)*
  5. **NO EVIDENCE-INTEGRITY SIDE UNITS unless a mismatch actually BLOCKS a Jay decision.** *(The F1-width-bar re-shoot was correct and useful — it disproved the review's diagnosis — but nothing turned on it.)*
  6. **SCOUTS STAY — they were the best value of the round.** Six read-only scouts: W-37 closed a whole unit, W-35b closed another, the d=5 question was settled and the follow-up's design improved, the tone-vacuity sweep returned a clean zero, and the moiré scout is running now. **Each cost one read-only pass and several replaced a planner, an implementer and a review.**
  ⚠ **What this does NOT relax: every standing ruling above, and `ROUND3-RESUME-BRIEFS.md` §0 in full.** The bars, the mutation proofs, the fixture statements and the disclosure rules are what made the round's findings findable. **This trims the CEREMONY around units, not the RIGOUR inside them.**

- **EVERY REPORTED NUMBER STATES THE FIXTURE IT WAS MEASURED ON — RIG, CAMERA, DENSITY, AND EVERY NON-DEFAULT PARAM — IN THE REPORT AND IN `report.json`** (2026-09-13, promoted after THREE script-methodology discrepancies in a single round, all in otherwise-rigorous reports). **Rule 3 ("name the rig") caught the rig and none of these three.** The three: **(a)** `trochoidLoop` ink −0.98 % vs −1.10 % on md5-identical source — a measurement slip in one script, unresolvable until someone re-ran the committed source directly; **(b)** `onePenDown` width 0.9860 vs 1.0089 mm, blamed on an intervening fix and actually **pre-dating it entirely** — a pure script-methodology difference; **(c)** F1-width-bar's own evidence shot at **`fillAngle: 45`** while every measured number used no `fillAngle`, a **~35–40 % `inkMm` divergence between the pictures and the table in the same report.** ⚠ **All three were found by a reviewer re-deriving from scratch, and none was detectable from the report alone — because the report named a number without naming what produced it.** **A number without its fixture is not reproducible, and "md5-identical source" is not a fixture.** ⚠ **AND STATE WHETHER GROUND-PLANE INK IS INCLUDED IN ANY INK TOTAL** (added 2026-09-13, when the re-shoot proved **`fillAngle` absent ≡ 45** and found the real cause of a ~35–40 % divergence was **ground-plane ink counted in one measurement and not the other**). **Two ink numbers for "the same cell" can differ by a third with both being correct and neither being wrong — the object's ink and the scene's ink are different quantities, and nothing in a bare `inkMm` says which one you have.**

- **A COVERAGE METRIC WHOSE CELL IS LARGER THAN THE DEFECT CANNOT SEE THE DEFECT — STATE YOUR METRIC'S RESOLUTION** (2026-09-13, from T2-3's plan; the audit's TENTH oracle-instrument finding). **Both rejected T2 attempts reported site-level coverage of 0.974–0.993 and both were rejected for bare wedges** — because **a 0.8 mm tick inside a 4.43 mm cross-row cell counts as COVERED.** The metric was real and honestly measured; **its cell was five times the size of the thing it was being trusted to find.** ⚠ **This is distinct from the vacuity findings: nothing here passes when it should fail. The metric answers a genuine question — it is simply not the question anyone was asking.** **Rule: when a metric aggregates over cells, bins or samples, state the cell size next to the number, and check it against the scale of the defect you are claiming to exclude. If the defect is smaller than the cell, the metric is silent, not reassuring.**

- **A FIX WHOSE DEFECT NO BAR MEASURES SHIPS ITS INSTRUMENT IN THE SAME UNIT — the bar is a PRECONDITION of the fix, not a follow-up** (2026-09-13, ruled forward on T2-3 before its own verdict was in, so the scope could not drift afterwards). **It follows directly from the "which half do you gate" rule: a fix for a defect nothing measures cannot state which half of the acceptance bar it gates, so it cannot be reviewed under that rule at all.** ⚠ **The pattern is now twice-proven and both times the instrument was a PICTURE: ribbon width, unmeasured, caught by the orchestrator's eye → §4 decision 10 and F1-width-bar; bare-wedge area, unmeasured, caught the same way → T2-3.** **When a picture finds what no bar can, the missing bar is part of the defect.**
- **A POPULATION OR FIXTURE CHANGE IS A BAR CHANGE** (2026-09-13; now in `AGENT-PROTOCOL.md` §Bars changed and `ROUND3-RESUME-BRIEFS.md` §0 rule 6). **Narrowing the population an assertion measures over hides a defect exactly as a widened tolerance does, and "the number is unchanged" is not a defence.** *(T2-2 re-scoped O1 from all walked ticks to the longest third while keeping `>= 0.10 mm`. The re-scope is sound and was independently ruled so twice — but it was disclosed only because that implementer chose to; the old wording did not require it.)*

- **A BAR THAT CLAIMS "TONE IS PRESERVED" MUST BE MEASURED AT d=50, NOT d=220 — AT d=220 TONE HAS NO AUTHORITY** (2026-09-13, from W-31b's Rank-3 C7 guard, mutation-proved). **W-36c's anti-saturation cap binds at maximum density**, which is exactly what Jay's decision 6 = C asked for and it stays — **but it means the tone law cannot move the result there, so any assertion of the form "tone is still preserved at d=220" is VACUOUS: it would pass with the tone mechanism removed.** ⚠ **This is the NINTH oracle-instrument finding of the audit and the first where a CORRECT, DELIBERATE fix silently voided a family of unrelated claims** — the cap is right, the bars built on top of it were measuring nothing, and **nobody noticed until a plan went looking for a different defect.** **Rule: measure tone authority at d=50. And when auditing an existing suite, treat every d=220 tone claim as suspect until re-measured — the vacuity is invisible from inside the assertion, which passes.**

- **WHEN A SWEEP REPORTS A CHANGED LAW, GREP FOR PINS ON THAT LAW AND CHECK THE FIXTURE EACH PIN USES — NOT JUST THE LAW NAME** (2026-09-12, promoted from F1-erode's condition-5 near-miss). The reviewer grepped every test for a numeric or md5 pin on the three laws the sweep reported gaining ink and found exactly one candidate: `scene3d-curved-density-sparse-end.test.js:329`, an md5 pin on `hatch/taperedEnds`. **It survived only because its fixture is the SPHERE, while the cell that actually moved was the CONE** — 20/20 both trees, file byte-identical, no re-pin needed. **A law-name match is a HIT LIST, not a verdict**: the pin and the changed cell must agree on primitive, mapper AND density before the pin is at risk, and they must agree before you may declare it safe either. **Both errors are live** — re-pinning a guard that was never threatened is the same class of damage as missing one that was, and this audit has done the first at least twice. **Read the fixture the pin actually constructs, not the `test.each` row's label.**

- **STATE A SWEEP'S COVERAGE AS A FRACTION OF THE ROSTER, AND JUSTIFY EVERY EXCLUSION** (2026-09-12, from F1-erode). **A planner's honestly-scoped sweep missed a live instance of the very defect it was scoping.** `F1-erode-plan.md` swept `hatch` on four primitives plus `contour` on the torus and reported a blast radius; the implementer, told to extend it to all **8 mappers × torus × 37 laws (296 cells)**, found **three more changed cells — one of them `crosshatch/interlockWeave` with `erodeEmpty` 1 → 0, a SECOND live instance of the same bug.** **Sweep breadth is itself a measurement, not a convenience.** Every plan and every impl report that claims a blast radius must state **what fraction of the roster it covered** and **why each exclusion is safe** — and "the other mappers are unrelated" is a claim to be measured, not assumed. Note the cheap confirmation available here: `none`, `wireframe`, `spiral`, `stipple` and `contourSlice` changed **zero** cells across all 37 laws, so the full sweep cost little and bought a finding.

- **STATE WHICH HALF OF THE ACCEPTANCE BAR A GUARD ACTUALLY GATES — AND MUTATION-TEST THE HALF YOU CLAIM** ⚠ **— AND THE MUTATION PROOF IS A BLOCKING REVIEW CONDITION, ruled 2026-09-12 on F1-erode's own three new gates** (`erodeEmpty === 0`, `swallowedAndEmpty === 0`, `wide`/`ribbons` equality). **"It is an integer gate where only the fixed value passes" is an ARGUMENT, not a proof** — the same shape as every claim this round that did not survive re-measurement. A reviewer may **REJECT** on a missing mutation proof for a bar the unit claims to gate. (2026-09-12; promoted after it produced THREE findings in one day). A guard that moves in the right *direction* is not a guard on the *defect*. Today: **T4b's band has a mathematically dead lower half** (`BASELINE × 0.9 = 1350.96 < 1400`, the floor), **and its live upper half is structurally blind to the slab** — the slab-regrowing formula measures **byte-identical ink at the guarded cell** and only shows at d=1 (+167 %), so T4b gates the ink half of Jay's decision-1 bar and not the "no slab defect" half, while reading as if it gated both. **W-36d's restored sub-bar misses interior-only degradations** — two mutations touching no sampled point pass every bar in the file. **And F1-placement's nine reds measure coverage, fill rate, residue and gap geometry while the thing that visibly changed was ribbon WIDTH**, which no bar measures at all. **The rule:** when a unit's acceptance bar has more than one clause, **name the clause your guard covers, name the clauses it does not, and prove the covered one with a mutation that trips it.** This is sharper than "ask what quantity the bar measures": that asks whether the instrument is right, **this asks how much of the promise it keeps.** A guard whose title implies the whole bar and whose assertions cover half of it is how a fixed defect comes back.

- **"RESTATED" means a GATE, never a retirement** (2026-09-12, ruled on F1-placement condition 3). When a
  ruled condition says a bar is **restated on a different quantity**, the unit must **gate that quantity**,
  with pre- and post-fix numbers measured **on its own tree**. The only honest alternative is a **measured
  stop-report on that single condition, disclosed under `## Bars changed`** — ship the numbers and say the
  metric does not separate defect from fix. **Silently retiring a ruled condition to "measured, not gated"
  REJECTS the unit.** The plan's own rule still binds in the other direction: *do not restate a bar on a
  quantity you have not first shown RED at.* The two together mean a restatement either produces a gate with
  a RED behind it, or a disclosed stop-report — never a quiet downgrade.
- **"Pre-existing" is a MEASUREMENT, never an inheritance — and an env-gated pre-fix comparison must be
  proven to gate the file it is run against** (2026-09-12; the ruling below survives, its F1-placement
  application does not). **Within hours of being filed, this ruling's own test case was disproved by the
  discipline it demands.** The dead F1-placement implementer claimed f1b-streaks 8/44 and wall-coverage were
  pre-existing, "identical to 13 decimal places" pre and post; it had set `VECTURA_PRE_F1P=1`, but
  **f1b-streaks reads `VECTURA_PRE_F1B` (an unrelated baseline) and wall-coverage reads no flag at all** —
  so both runs ran **identical post-fix code**. A true `git archive` export of the base sha measures
  **44/44 and 36/36, clean.** **Before trusting any env-gated "pre-fix" run, grep the target file for the
  flag name and confirm the fix's own symbol is absent from the tree you are calling pre-fix.** When in
  doubt, `git archive` the base sha — comparing a thing to itself and calling the match proof of no
  regression is the vacuous-proof class W-38 was rejected for, now shipped three times in this audit.
- **Pre-existing red is not a blocker, but it must be PINNED and NAMED** (2026-09-12, ruled on
  fill-audit-a3's `scene3d-ribbon-f1b-streaks.test.js` 8/44 and `scene3d-ribbon-wall-coverage.test.js` —
  ⚠ **that specific application is WITHDRAWN, because the red turned out to be caused by the fix; the rule
  itself STANDS and is vindicated, since requiring the base-sha reproduction is exactly what exposed the
  false premise**). A
  unit may proceed over redness it did not cause, on two conditions: **(a) the failing set is IDENTICAL at
  the base sha and at the final commit** — same files, same tests, same numbers — and **(b) it is named in
  the implementer's report under a heading `## Pre-existing red`**, test by test. **Unit A's five
  intentionally-red streak tests are known and expected; the other three must be named individually.** An
  "it was already broken" with no enumerated set is a hidden regression.
- **A copy-only unit may skip `npm run test:ci`** (2026-09-12, ruled on U7-2, on the U7/U8 precedent) —
  **on condition the REVIEWER runs, in its own post export, the targeted collapse / picker / caveat files
  plus the generative cross-check, and re-sums the headline itself.** **Full `test:ci` belongs to the
  merge**, not to a unit whose diff is confined to data/config/test files with zero render change.
- **A unit that turns another unit's test GREEN must SAY SO in its report** (2026-09-11, from U6-2 folding
  into U9b). U9b now carries U6-2's fix for `scene3d-fill-style-display-params.test.js`, a file U9b did not
  make red. **A file that goes red → green inside a unit that did not cause the red reads as "all tests pass"
  at merge and hides which unit owns the fix.** Name the borrowed green explicitly.
- **The generative cross-check is part of EVERY fold's scope, not a guard that happens to be nearby**
  (2026-09-11, after three occurrences in one lane). `tests/unit/scene3d-fill-style-display-params.test.js`
  (W-10d-3's, pinned by U5b-3) **does not survive a roster change without being extended** — U7 and U8 each
  had to touch it and **U6 broke it outright**. Any future fold that changes the roster **extends that
  cross-check in the same unit**, or it ships green and the next fold inherits a red file it did not cause.
- **Lane ownership follows OBSERVABILITY, not just file ownership** (2026-09-09, ruled on W-30d, overruling the
  secretary). W-30d is `shadows.js` work and handoff-c2 owns `shadows.js` — but **handoff-c2 does not contain
  W-30b or W-30c**, so the torus footprint through the wired projector **cannot be observed in the running app
  there**, and "no user-visible change is done until observed in the running app" is non-negotiable. **A lane
  that cannot show the fix cannot own it.** W-30d therefore runs on **fill-collapse-2, serialized after U8**,
  with handoff-c consulted. **General form for the rest of the round: when file ownership and observability
  point at different lanes, observability wins and the file conflict becomes a merge note.**
- **W-31: run Rank 1's go/no-go SPIKE (§4.1a) before committing to Rank 1** (2026-09-09, the planner's own
  recommendation, adopted). ⚠ **The plan discloses, deliberately, that Rank 1 was NOT prototyped** — it is argued
  from the mechanism, whereas Rank 2 and Rank 3 were both built and measured, and whereas **W-36's plan shipped a
  prototyped Rank 1**. That difference is the reason for the spike. **If the spike clears the cylinder band, take
  Rank 1** (per-sample "warped" offset placement, gated to the crosshatch pair — ruling i+1 becomes the offset
  curve of ruling i at the locally-wanted clearance, so the cell is square by construction, and it discharges
  (c-2) as a side effect by making the tone target per-sample). **If it does not, take Rank 3** — land the
  cell-shape test as a **non-regression ceiling at today's numbers** (nothing tightened, nothing widened) and
  file the placement rewrite as **W-31b**. **Never take Rank 2** (per-sample tone only): it was prototyped and
  **REJECTED by measurement** — do not retry it.
- **Serialization-map amendment for W-30c's duration** (2026-09-09, the secretary's region flag adopted):
  **`src/core/algorithms/scene3d.js`'s shadow/footprint region (`:1429-1436`, `:2496-2503`) and
  `src/core/scene3d/regions.js` belong to fill-collapse-2** while W-30c runs. `scene3d.js` now has **three**
  named regions with three different owners — the faceted path (fill-audit-2), the slices pass
  (fill-audit-d2) and the shadow/footprint block (fill-collapse-2) — so "scene3d.js is taken" and
  "scene3d.js is free" are both wrong answers. Always name the region.
- **The `params.js` hold on W-10d-2 is LIFTED** (2026-09-09), and the ledger had the wrong claimant for two
  days. `git diff --stat 47a5a755..HEAD -- src/core/scene3d/params.js` on **fill-collapse-2 is EMPTY**; the
  real contention was always **handoff-c2** (U9, `+37/-3` in the shadow bag). The ROUND2-BRIEFS line

  block; failing that proof is PLAN-BLOCKED.
- **W-30c lands on fill-collapse-2, serialized AFTER U7 closes** (2026-09-09), with handoff-c consulted as
  the `shadows.js` owner — mirroring the W-30b ruling rather than inventing a new arrangement. Verified basis:
  **handoff-c2 has no `shadows.js` edits** (its diff vs main is `params.js`, two test files, an audit script
  and evidence), so there is no conflict on that file. ⚠ **Secretary note for the implementer: W-30c's F2/F3
  touch a THIRD region of `src/core/algorithms/scene3d.js`** — the shadow/footprint code at `:1429-1436` and
  `:2496-2503` — which is neither fill-audit-2's faceted path nor fill-audit-d2's slices pass. The
  serialization map has only ever named two regions of that file; **name the third explicitly in the brief**
  so a future lane does not assume the file is free. F1's file, `src/core/scene3d/regions.js`, appears in no
  lane's ownership list at all and should be claimed by this unit.
- **RECORD CORRECTION (2026-09-08, W-10d-3 planner, measured): the ledger's and STILL-OPEN's account of
  W-10d-3's root cause was WRONG.** Both said `_composeSceneGroup` "feeds `algo.generate()` without ever
  calling `normalizeParams`/`sanitizeSceneParams`". Measured: **`algo.generate` calls `normalizeParams`
  itself** (`scene3d.js:1191`), so the render is always normalized — which is why the canvas has been right
  all along. **The true defect is narrower: normalization is applied to a THROW-AWAY COPY and never written
  back to `layer.params`, and both UI surfaces read the layer bag.** The reachable load channel is
  **`engine.js:422`** (object3d leaves get `migrateScene` only), **not** the compose call. Cite this version.
- **W-10d-3's `displayParams` COMPLETES U5b rather than duplicating it — and it exposes a live hole in U5b**
  (2026-09-08, measured). `effectiveLaw(survivor, bag)` reconstructs the folded id **from the bag's sibling
  key**; on a RAW folded bag there is no sibling key, so `effectiveLaw('bundleCount', {toneLaw:'bundleDither'})`
  returns `'bundleCount'` and **U5b's caveat fix is silently defeated today** — the warning is still missing on
  exactly the documents U5b was written for. Stacking the Rank-1 fix under a simulation of U5b's hunks makes the
  caveat render **with zero extra code**, because `displayParams` hands `effectiveLaw` a seeded bag. **The
  implementer must NOT duplicate `effectiveLaw`.** ⚠ **Secretary condition:** `displayParams` is nonetheless a
  THIRD site encoding `ALIASES`→sibling-key knowledge (after `params.js`'s shim and `effectiveLaw`), so it must
  be added to **U5b-3's existing generative cross-check** (`tests/unit/scene3d-fill-style-effective-law.test.js`)
  rather than being given a private test — one cross-check pinning all three, or they drift apart the way U5b-3
  was created to prevent.
- **W-10d-3b filed (secretary, 2026-09-08)** — post-U9 the **shadow** picker is cosmetically imprecise: it shows
  `Ladder` for a stored `fineLadder` while `shadows.js:2713` renders `fineLadder`'s own recipe. That is a
  **one-key display question on a one-key bag**, adjacent to **U9b**, and is explicitly OUT of W-10d-3's scope —
  fixing it there would give the shadow bag the style bag's two-key shape, which is exactly what U9's reviewer
  forbids. **QUEUED on handoff-c2 with U9b**, not on fill-audit-2.
- **W-34 ships Fix A, plus Fix B only if its own T3 is RED; Fix C is Jay's** (2026-09-08).
  **Fix A — move the refinement's stop condition into DEVICE space** (7 lines: `refineSliceRing` takes an
  `opts.project`, `linkPlane` supplies the pass's own camera; subdivision and the Newton snap stay in world
  space). Prototyped: M1 cone **8.088 → 7.850**, capsule **9.075 → 7.928**, sphere 7.223 → 7.966, ellipsoid
  7.489 → 7.965, torus unchanged, cylinder 0 — **all six under 8**, 51/51 still green, and **all seven torus
  metrics byte-identical** while three sphere metrics improve. **Ship it framed as the honest close of the

  is true geometry. **Fix B — a closed form for `capsule` in `sliceSurfaceFG`** (one branch mirroring
  `charts.js topoCapsule`; every other primitive untouched by dispatch, and the capsule has no contourSlice
  assertion among the 51). **Land it only if T3 is RED — do NOT land it on the prediction.** If the capsule's
  analytic truth is itself ≈30°/mm at the cap/barrel seam, the excess is small and the report must say so.
  **Fix C — apex-band level warping on the cone — is FORBIDDEN to the implementer** and is filed as **§4
  decision 8 for Jay**: it is the only thing that removes the corner he circled, but it shifts **every** cone
  contourSlice ring (all 6 Tier-A + all 294 Tier-B cone cells), i.e. **a look change to a shipped treatment**,
  the same class as W-35. It must arrive with the plan's **before/after montage of cone planes 11–16**.
- **W-37 is the next free id** (secretary, 2026-09-08). W-34's §7.4 files it: **`GeometryUtils.toCurveAnchors`
  returns `{straight:true}` on every contourSlice ring measured, so Fill Curves is INERT on contourSlice
  output** even at the app default `fillCurves:true`. Rule 2b says rounding must not be off by default — here
  it is not off, it **declines**, which the rule never anticipated. `geometry-utils.js` is **outside
  fill-audit-d2's files**, so W-37 needs its own lane. File alongside it the degenerate stubs the same sweep
  found — zero-length 2-point paths (sphere 19, ellipsoid 8/19, torus 37, capsule 1/2/4/10/13/19/20) and
  3-point sub-millimetre remnants — which are the **W-29 family, not W-34**.
- **W-36's oracle is the planner's P1–P6, NOT the brief's literal ±10% count bar** (2026-09-08). The ±10%
  bar is **not reachable by any evenly-spaced placement** — equal pitch does not give equal counts, because the
  two families traverse different extents of the same silhouette (ellipsoid d=50 prototype: median gaps 2.902 vs
  2.893 mm, **0.3% apart**, but counts 9 vs 11, **22% apart**), and **the v1.3.98 picture Jay accepts as correct
  itself runs 0.54–1.10 on counts**. Forcing counts would require making one family unevenly spaced — exactly
  what **W-31 exists to forbid**. **Equal SPACING is the physical content of Jay's rule; count parity is its
  consequence up to the silhouette's aspect.** The oracle is therefore: **P1** both families ≥ 2 rulings on
  every cell · **P2** median-gap ratio B:A ∈ **[0.80, 1.25]** at d = 50/220 · **P3** count ratio B:A ∈
  **[0.72, 1.40]** at d = 50/220 · **P4** at d=1, `abs(nB − nA) ≤ 3` (integers, not a percentage) ·
  **P5** anti-saturation: cylinder d=220 crosshatch ink **≤ 5431 mm** (v1.3.98 + 15%) and ≥ 4200 mm ·
  **P6** byte-identity controls for hatch/contour (sphere hatch d=50 = 24 fills / 723.4 mm; cylinder hatch
  d=220 = 145 / 4495.5 mm; sphere contour d=50 = 18 / 656.4 mm). **This is a deviation from Jay's verbatim
  "same number of lines" and is filed for him as §4 decision 6** — it is not the secretary's or the
  orchestrator's to close.
- **W-36 ships Rank 1 first; Rank 2 is a visual-judgement fallback, never a silent one** (2026-09-08). Rank 1 =
  one **`CROSS_PAIR_BUDGET`** spent by the crossed PAIR and split by the dial — a **redistribution of the sum
  W-26b-1 already proved plot-safe, not new ink**. Rank 2 is the same mechanism at `CROSS_PAIR_BUDGET = 1.2`
  and costs **cylinder d=220 +16.1% vs v1.3.98 — just outside the ±15% cap** — so it may be taken **only** if
  the orchestrator looks at Rank 1's native-resolution crops and judges the crosshatch too light against Jay's
  reference, and then only with the W-26 conditional-acceptance ruling re-applied per delta, or Jay's call.
  **Rank 3 (`CROSS_SHARE_BASE` 0.1 → 1.0, the obvious one-liner) is REJECTED BY MEASUREMENT** — it gives the
  best count parity and **cylinder d=220 = 9136.8 mm, +93.5% vs v1.3.98**, restoring judge C1's saturation
  almost exactly (its own figure was 9326.7). It survives **only as the §3.2 mutation**; nobody re-tries it.

  below, which was unsafe as written**). Write back **only** for an explicitly named set of
  (primitive, mapper, law) combinations — today **exactly one: `torus` + any surface-fill mapper +
  `originSpiral`** — once, at document load and at the first unreachable `collectSceneParams`/compose.
  That combination's render **changes deliberately** (it is the repair W-10c/W-10d deferred) and
  **requires a CHANGELOG line**; **everything else stays byte-identical**, proven by a blast-radius
  negative (`sphere` + `spiral` + `taperedEnds` must come back unchanged in both the stored bag and the
  canvas). **No undo entry at load.** Guard G1 asserts the curated table has exactly one entry, so the
  set cannot widen unnoticed. **Contract B (render-safe) rejected** — byte-identical by construction but
  it *excludes the torus case* and those classes have no UI lie to fix, so the user value is ~zero.
  **Contract C (withdraw) rejected** — the torus repair is owed.
- **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-05 ruling, kept for the record (do NOT implement this):** "W-10d-2 resolves an
  unreachable `toneLaw` by WRITE-BACK, once… The target law is the picker's existing fallback choice —
  whatever `isReachableOn`'s consumers fall back to today." **Why it was wrong: it keyed the write-back
  on `isReachableOn`, which is not a safe engine-side oracle.**
- **STANDING NOTE — `isReachableOn` means "too degraded to be worth offering", NOT "draws nothing
  different"** (2026-09-09, measured by the W-10d-2 planner over all **35 `PICKER_IDS` × 10 primitives ×
  8 mappers = 2800 combinations**). **2014 / 2800 (71.9%) are `isReachableOn === false`**, and
  **1365 of those sit where `ladder` itself is unreachable** — every primitive under
  `none`/`wireframe`/`contourSlice`, every faceted primitive under `contour`/`spiral`/`stipple` — so
  **68% of the unreachable set has no fallback to write back to at all.** And the gate is not a no-op
  gate: faceted+hatch and cap-limited solids do render identically to `ladder`, but curved+spiral,
  curved+stipple, pyramid+spiral and the torus gate all render **visibly differently**
  (`torus`/`hatch`/`originSpiral` = 556 paths / 5851 pts vs ladder's 135 / 910). W-03's own comment says
  it out loud: its 36 inert-list laws are "18-19 distinct pictures across 39 laws". **Therefore a blanket
  `isReachableOn`-keyed write-back would silently rewrite the stored fill style of MOST objects in the
  corpus and change every curved+spiral/stipple render — that is data loss, not a migration.** Any future
  unit tempted to use this gate as a "safe to substitute" predicate must re-read this note first.

## Cross-cutting watch list (secretary checks on every report)

- ⚠ **The row-lattice light-block pattern is FILED as T2-3e (row 5a-4), a round-4 item, and DISCLOSED to Jay in his T2 row** — so the shipped pictures carry a KNOWN, NAMED artefact rather than an unowned one. **Not a new pattern and not worsened by T2-3b: period ≈40 px pre / ≈47 px post, contrast 17.66 pre / 16.80 post — within noise.**

- No `git push`, no merge, no cross-lane edits (serialization map in `AGENT-PROTOCOL.md`).
- No widened tolerance, no fingerprint re-pin without before/after counts in the commit body.
- `after` paths must resolve to `after/<W-id>/…`, never `shots/`; byte-identical pairs must be explained.
- Implementer must state what it SAW in the PNGs; harness-clean ≠ app-clean.
- Vacuous-pass guards (paths>0, weightScale===1, before/after not byte-identical).
- Version bump hook cannot fire in a worktree — a bump in a lane commit is an error.

   d=10 4→7; crosshatch 23,8→31,14). c861bf97's body is only the bare "agent died at the API session
   limit" WIP note; 9fa159f0 is docs-only. No commit body carries the re-pin proof the protocol demands.
2. **Oracle weaker than the worklist `done_when`.** W-01's done_when says path counts *strictly increase*
   across d=1/10/25/50. The new "literal checkpoints (M1/M2)" block asserts counts **non-decreasing** and
   only *ink* strictly increasing — which is what lets torus+hatch+fineLadder tie 6==6 at d=10/25.
3. **Snapshot-style pins.** That block also hard-pins `expect(counts).toEqual(...)` and
   `expect(ink).toEqual(...)`, i.e. it records whatever the tree emits. Mutation-sensitivity must be shown
   (fail at boost=6), not assumed.
4. **RED proof used `git stash` in the shared worktree** — the protocol forbids stash-based red proofs and
   mandates a scratch `git archive` export. Tree is clean now, so it appears popped; confirm no orphan
   stash and re-derive RED from an export of c861bf97^.
5. **Byte-identity narrative needs independent md5s.** One md5 (`14b9a50d…`) is cited as before-low,

6. **Evidence coverage gap.** Unit md5 guards cover d=50 and d=220; the image evidence covers sphere med

from 78bbf3e8 rather than reused from `shots/`; no byte-identical before→after pair; worktree clean.
Flags to forward:
1. **Scope reframing (implementer-flagged, still needs a ruling).** Shipped mechanism is
   tone-by-REPETITION — the same floor-safe ring is retraced `L-1` extra times over flagged arcs — not
   the brief's tone-by-OMISSION (skip every k-th turn). Decide whether double-inking one ring is an
   acceptable plotter outcome (ink blot, pen wear, doubled pen-down time) before accepting. Note:
   `src/core/optimization-utils.js` has no duplicate-path removal, so the retraces do survive export —

3. **`DUTY_CAP = 4` is close to binding** (implementer measured max wanted duty ~3.6) and no test asserts

4. **`localDuty = clamp(Math.round(PLOT_MIN_PEN / wanted), 1, DUTY_CAP)` rounds**, so an arc wanting duty

   "per-path-constant-width, no `meta.weightScale`" invariant is asserted in prose only — run

   implementer calls this pre-existing — verify against the pre-change `shots/B/` md5s, because if the new
   floor/duty path caused it, the 12-cell evidence set is really ~3 distinct images and the tonal-range

   what happens on **non-convergence** (no fallback observed), and the cost per ring point.

   (`scene3d.js:365,376`), and the new tests assert `|F|/|∇F| ≈ 1e-9…1e-12`. That asserts the loop
   converged, not that the point is the RIGHT point: nothing checks it stayed in the cutting plane, that
   it is the nearest surface point, or that the ring's radius matches the analytic circle. Plan R2 and
